- **ROOT CAUSE:** `decideClearRotation` (`src/main/supervisor/claude-clear-rotation.ts:31-35`) no-ops unless **exactly one** active claude agent shares the cwd slug AND it's this agent. === 🧭 FOLLOW-UP THREAD (2026-06-24) — stale per-workspace hook script `dashboard-status.mjs` ===
**Symptom:** the /clear bar-reset works in AgentDashboard ws but NOT some other ws. ROOT CAUSE: the feature needs the per-workspace hook script `.dashboard/scripts/dashboard-status.mjs` to emit `session_id` (line `record.sessionId = stdinMeta.session_id`, source `src/shared/constants.ts` ~:1367). Audited all ws on disk: STALE (missing line) = `JobHunt/Applications`, `JobHunt/JobSearch2.0`; all others HAVE it. The dashboard CODE is global (one main proc) but this SCRIPT is copied per-ws and drifts → stale copy = no candidate → rotation safely no-ops (frozen bar). Also explains the post-/clear chat-panel bug (user msg sourced from stale session reader; assistant text via separate live path).
**RESOLVED DIAGNOSIS (worker `b70e7583`, 2026-06-24):** my "stale-forever" hypothesis was WRONG. `dashboard-status.mjs` IS version-managed + self-healing — it's `WORKSPACE_SCRIPT_FILES` (`index.ts:1711`, `version:9`, `previousHashes` 1–8) written via `writeScaffoldMap` (same engine as settings.json). REAL BUG = **launch-trigger gap**: `WORKSPACE_SCRIPT_FILES` is written ONLY by persona launch (`index.ts:1577`) + `ensureWorkerScaffold` (`:1832`); NOT by `ensureSupervisorScaffold` (`:1809`) or `ensureResearcherScaffold` (`:1898`) — yet supervisor + researcher both depend on the shared script for their own status hooks. So a ws that's only launched supervisors/researchers since the last script bump stays stale until a worker/persona launches there. Sidecar evidence: AgentDashboard=9 (works), JobHunt/Applications=5, JobHunt/JobSearch2.0=0 (stale, inert). v7 added `session_id` capture (constants.ts:1387).
  - **✅ FIXED — Option B (Edward picked B; worker `b70e7583`, 2026-06-24).** `launchAgent` now calls a new private `ensureWorkspaceScripts(workDir, pathType)` → `writeScaffoldMap(WORKSPACE_SCRIPT_FILES)` ONCE before the lane if/else, so every lane (supervisor/researcher/worker/persona) version-migrates the shared scripts (`dashboard-status.mjs` AND `read-comments.py`) before lane dispatch. Per-lane writes become no-op skips (sidecar==bundled; proven by test B3, mtime unchanged). GREEN: build:main clean; scaffold-version-migration 24/0 (new B1 missing→written, B2 stale-hash→silent upgrade, B3 current→no-op, B4 edited→.bak+overwrite; B1 also asserts pre-fix gap); scaffold-writer 4/0; worker-scaffold 8/0; dashboard-status-script 18/0. UNCOMMITTED, not restarted. JobHunt/Applications (v5→silent) + JobHunt/JobSearch2.0 (v0→.bak+overwrite) self-heal on next launch of ANY lane once shipped.
  - **WORKING TREE now has TWO distinct uncommitted changes:** (1) /clear bar-reset hook-bound disambiguation (workers df096170→4bea3254); (2) this scaffold launch-trigger fix (b70e7583). Consider separate commits. Edward to restart + live-verify both; commit = his call.

=== 🧭 RESUME HERE (2026-06-19 NEWEST+3) — MULTI-SUPERVISOR-AS-CARDS blocker root-caused (relay from Mr Job Hunt) + identity-bleed link ===
**Trigger:** Edward reached "Mr Job Hunt" (privilege-lane persona, JobHunt ws) via the workspace **Supervisor button**, believing it was the generic Supervisor. Investigating that identity bleed surfaced the SAME blocker as "supervisors should be their own cards, not a single header button." Mr Job Hunt did NOT edit any files; relayed the finding. **I VERIFIED all cited lines against live source — accurate.**
**ROOT CAUSE — `renderer/stores/dashboard-store.ts:846-848` (`loadAgents`):**
```
const agents = allAgents.filter((a) => !a.isSupervisor);            // supervisors STRIPPED from grid
const supervisorAgent = allAgents.find((a) => a.isSupervisor) || null;  // collapsed to ONE nullable singleton
```
- `filter(!isSupervisor)` → a supervisor structurally CANNOT be a card (AgentGrid only renders `agents`).
- `find(isSupervisor)` → all supervisors collapse to ONE store field `supervisorAgent: Agent|null` = workspace singleton.
- **+SECOND FILTER SITE I found (Mr Job Hunt missed it): `loadAllAgents` `dashboard-store.ts:854` ALSO `filter((a)=>!a.isSupervisor)`** — must be fixed too or the all-workspaces view keeps hiding supervisors.
- That singleton renders as HEADER CHROME in `renderer/components/layout/MainContent.tsx:136-200` (purple "Supervisor" button → `launchSupervisor(workspace.id)`; status pill + ctx bar + reset-X when running). One supervisor per ws BY CONSTRUCTION.
**IDENTITY-BLEED LINK:** `find(a=>a.isSupervisor)` returns the FIRST flagged agent. On the LIVE app the decouple fix (`85f7a102`, sets persona `privilegeLane:'supervisor'`+`isSupervisor:false`) is GREEN-ON-DISK but **NOT restarted** → live persona still launches `isSupervisor:true` → it WINS the singleton slot over the real `.dashboard/supervisor` → Supervisor button routed to the persona. **`npm run restart` (folds in 85f7a102) makes the persona stop competing for the slot** even before the bigger refactor.
**DATA MODEL ALREADY HALF-MIGRATED to target (`shared/types.ts`):** `AgentRoleLane='legacy'|'supervisor'|'worker'|'researcher'`; `privilegeLane?:'supervisor'` (comment: "isSupervisor stays false so it renders as its own card"); `AgentCard.tsx` ALREADY has a Supervisor role chip (~L301) AND privilege-lane/"Elevated" chip (~L316). Card rendering + lane vocab EXIST — blocker is PURELY the store singleton + grid filter + header button still on the old `isSupervisor` boolean.

**Trigger:** Mr Job Hunt persona (JobHunt ws, agent `0ebb8439`) ran a LIVE end-to-end test of its real flow — it runs `JobSearchInteractive/Scripts/scheduler.py` from its **Bash** tool; that pure-Python script drives the dashboard via the **HTTP API** (POST /api/agents etc.), NOT MCP tools. Every write 401'd: "Missing or invalid API token." It surfaced 3 blockers; I VERIFIED all against source:
- **BLOCKER #1 (NEW, FOUNDATIONAL) — token doesn't reach agent Bash. CONFIRMED.** Token is injected ONLY into the **MCP sidecar's** env block in the `--mcp-config` JSON (`index.ts:2135-2158`, `mcp-config-builder.ts:199-216`). The AGENT's process env never gets it → its Bash (child of agent process) inherits nothing → shell-spawned scripts can't authenticate. MCP tools work because the sidecar holds the token; Bash is downstream of the agent process, not the sidecar. Agent spawn sites: `windows-runner.ts:~89` (`sanitizeClaudeChildEnv({...process.env, ...extraEnv})`) and `wsl-runner.ts:~567` (WSL also needs WSLENV to cross into Linux — pattern at `index.ts:3378-3382`).
- **BLOCKER #2 — no owner edge. CONFIRMED.** Zero `owner_agent_id`/`parent_agent_id`/`launched_by` in src. (Unchanged from original plan.)
- **BLOCKER #3 — events logged not delivered; `worker_stalled` "missing". CONFIRMED w/ CORRECTION.** `worker_stalled` DOES exist + DOES deliver (`event-bridge.ts:418`, `event-payload-builder.ts:290/340`, stall-watchdog sink `status-monitor.ts:1063-1102`) — but ONLY to the structural supervisor (gated `!isSupervised` + `getSupervisorForWorker`→the one structural supervisor), and it's a `supervisor_event` injection, NOT a row in the `events` table (that's why Mr Job Hunt's DB query found none + he received none). So the gap is **TARGET RESOLUTION**, not "build worker_stalled". Matches the original lead-planner finding.
**KEY MECHANISM (settles the "why GroupThink works but scheduler.py doesn't" question Edward asked):** 3 channels — (1) **GroupThink** runs IN-PROCESS inside the dashboard: `orchestration/dashboard-client.ts` calls `supervisor.launchAgent()`/`sendInput()` directly, no HTTP/token, trusted by construction ("the same calls the HTTP routes wrap"). (2) **MCP tools** = privileged **sidecar** process holding the token. (3) **scheduler.py in Bash** = child of agent process, no token → 401. The HTTP API exists so OUT-OF-PROCESS code can do what GroupThink does in-process; scheduler.py is a legit consumer, only missing credential delivery.
**EDWARD'S DECISION (2026-06-19):** UNBLOCK FAST = Option A. "If we're giving this agent supervisor-level permissions, its Bash should have it too." Whatever credential level an agent has, carry it into its subprocesses. External materials will be QUARANTINED in future → token-in-env leak risk accepted. Applies to Mr Job Hunt + all future privileged agents.

=== 🧭 RESUME HERE (2026-06-19 NEWEST) — AGENT-OWNERSHIP PRIMITIVE (the real goal) + the provider-launch bug that derailed the first GroupThink ===

**THE ACTUAL FEATURE WE'RE BUILDING (do not lose this — it's the headline ask, currently PAUSED behind a bug fix):**
**AGENT-OWNERSHIP / IDENTITY-ISOLATION PRIMITIVE.** Edward created a user persona "Mr Job Hunt" with supervisor-tier tools in the JobHunt ws. It launches researcher agents — directly via `launch_agent` MCP AND via a pure-Python script `JobHunt/JobSearchInteractive/Scripts/scheduler.py` (POSTs `/api/agents`). PROBLEM: a child launched by an agent has **no ownership edge back to its launcher**. Confirmed: NO `owner_agent_id`/`parent_agent_id`/`launched_by` column anywhere in src. So child status/idle/done events route to the structural Supervisor lane and **never reach the agent that launched the child**.
- **Goal contract:** durable owner edge on BOTH launch paths — (1) direct MCP `launch_agent` → dashboard sets owner = calling agent automatically; (2) script-on-behalf POST `/api/agents` → owner forwarded via an injected env var `AGENT_DASHBOARD_SELF_ID` the parent's shell passes into the POST body (mirrors how scheduler.py already inherits `AGENT_DASHBOARD_API_TOKEN`). Then **deliver** child status/idle/stall/done/context-threshold events into the OWNER's live input (`POST /api/agents/:owner/input` as `[DASHBOARD EVENT]`), not just a `supervisor_event` row. Edge cases: owner busy/compacted/forked/dead, owner≠supervisor, multiple supervisors per ws each owning their own children, ownership inheritance on fork.
- **KEY GROUPTHINK FINDING (lead planner, before run died — REFRAMES the work):** `worker_stalled` + `context_threshold` event types ALREADY EXIST and already deliver into the supervisor's live session (event-bridge.ts:406/432, status-monitor.ts:1069, event-payload-builder). The real gap is **TARGET RESOLUTION**: `getSupervisorForWorker` (index.ts:947) hard-resolves to the single structural `getSupervisorAgent(workspaceId)`, and `onStatusChanged` gates on `!agent.isSupervised`. So an owned-but-non-supervisor child's events are DROPPED. Design ≈ add owner column + swap delivery target from "the ws's one supervisor" to "the resolved owner"; replace the `!isSupervised` gate with "resolvable owner OR isSupervised". `owner_kind` ('mcp'|'script') optional for observability.
- **DEEPER (surfaced by the incident below):** this is ALSO **agent identity isolation** — agents in a lane SHARE one cwd (`.dashboard/workers/<provider>`) with no strong per-agent identity binding, so cwd-based codex session recovery can cross-bind one agent to another's session. Ownership sits ON TOP of identity isolation; the expanded design must cover per-agent cwd / unstealable session binding.
- **Plan was:** GT-1 design→`plans/agent-ownership-primitive-design.md`, then GT-2 impl breakdown. GT-1 run `ad905d0d` (serial, claude lead `d45deeaa` + codex reviewer `d67daba2`) **ABORTED** (see below); plan file never written.

**WHY GT-1 DIED — TWO BUGS, ROOT-CAUSED (Edward: "forget the original plan, fix this first"):**
- **BUG #1 (PRIMARY) — provider override silently discarded → codex/gemini launch the CLAUDE binary.** `index.ts:1236-1238`: `isFrameworkDefault` is EXACT-equality vs `DEFAULT_COMMAND`/`DEFAULT_COMMAND_WSL`. Every workspace's STORED `default_command` is the legacy `claude --dangerously-skip-permissions --chrome` (WSL: `ccode … --chrome`). Edward stripped `--chrome` from the framework-default CONSTANTS but the stored DB rows were never migrated → `stored ≠ default` everywhere → `isFrameworkDefault=false` → stored claude command used verbatim for EVERY provider → `defaultCmd` (the provider-correct codex cmd) discarded. Claude agents stay fine (the `--chrome` token is stripped at runtime, index.ts:1270); **non-claude agents silently become claude.** App-wide, all 8 workspaces. This is why GT-1's "codex" reviewer was actually Claude Code (terminal showed "Opus 4.8", `/btw`, `/reload-plugins`).

**Commits:** S1 777b993 · S2 d70e2b3 · S3 ec679b6 · S4 f006f7d · S5 2eb78b0 · (openUrl fix 6b32439) · S6 bd61bb2 · S7 83dd751 · S8 142fb8d · S9 0c5cc52 · S10+11 2b08577 · S12 0d8a412 · S13 ea92fd3 · S14 242102b · S15 0693bd2 · S16 7360498. Baseline was 9b147a8.
**Final state: working tree CLEAN except this MEMORY.md. Every slice gated (full build + node+vitest suites green) before its single commit. NOT YET human-reviewed / NOT restarted into the live app — present to Edward for review.**
**🔑 LOAD-BEARING LESSONS for future multi-slice autonomous runs (proven across this run):**
1. Cross-cutting main+renderer slices (10+11, 12, 13, 14, 15) reliably burn a single fresh worker on READING before it writes. WINNING PATTERN = split MAIN worker (does logic+IPC+tests, FORBIDDEN renderer reads) → reports its IPC/event surface in the patch summary → RENDERER worker consumes that surface INLINE (FORBIDDEN main reads). Pure-renderer (16) or pure-polish slices = one worker but ordered checklist + stop-at-75%.
2. BUILD-FIRST forcing function ("run build:main, let the compiler flag the gaps") cracks a half-written file faster than re-reading it — but only helps when there's a red build / stubs (no help for purely-additive green code).
3. STOP-FIRST → SNAPSHOT-AFTER → THEN brief the replacement. A dying worker keeps writing in its final tool calls, so a diff taken BEFORE stopping is stale → caused 3 near-collisions where I'd reassign files the dead worker had just written. Always re-snapshot disk AFTER the stop.
4. Don't compact a worker that's PRODUCTIVE + build-green just because a threshold fired — let it finish; if it dies, the work is on disk and the supervisor can run the gate + commit directly (no continuation needed, e.g. S16).
5. Commit cadence: one clean commit per green slice by EXPLICIT source paths, MEMORY.md kept out, Co-author Claude Opus 4.8.
**FOLLOW-UPS (non-blocking, for human):** (a) reader extraction is heuristic (no Readability dep) — output may be thin on some sites; adding @mozilla/readability would enrich it. (b) discardThresholdMs renderer mirror defaults 30min — main has no getter; add one if the displayed value must reflect main's real default. (c) pre-existing `tsc -p tsconfig.json` warnings (notebook/NewTabPage.test + sessionRestore guard) unrelated to this run. (d) needs a human `npm run restart` + manual viewport/visual pass (S16 motion, overlay occlusion at narrow/medium/wide).
**Decision gate note (S13):** downloads APPROVED+EXTENDED by Edward 2026-06-17 — agents may download from allowlisted origins only; full audit; M12 sensitive-origin discipline still applies.

- _(superseded) prior HALF B worker `6e66f25a`:_ access-policy-store lastSignedInAt/lastUsedAt/verifiedAt; manager completeSigninHandoff success + off-origin revoke event + lastUsedAt; SigninHandoffBanner success+Cancel-handoff+quarantine badge; WebsiteAccessSettings "Sessions shared with agents" section (Return/Clear/Disable + amber expired chip + confirm on Clear-agent-session + pending-row age/agent-name); BrowserTabStrip toolbar "Agent driving" chip. === 🧪 SYSTEM-TEST FINDING (2026-06-17) — site-access APPROVAL does not NOTIFY the requesting agent (researcher gave up on native browsing) ===
**Repro:** researcher 59276984 (Guerneville-hotel task) hit the agent-browsing gate, used the §18 request-and-approve flow, human APPROVED in the UI (worked) → BUT the researcher got no signal the grant landed, so it abandoned `browser_open_url` and fell back to WebFetch (which only returns JS-rendered empty shells for booking engines → useless for live availability/rates). I could not nudge it immediately: `send_message_to_agent` rejects while the agent is `working` (HANDSHAKE FAILED, state guard — nothing typed, no Enter to recover). Plan: nudge to retry native browser the moment it goes idle.
**GAP / FEATURE IDEA (user-raised, worth a design pass):** approval is currently PULL-only — the agent must re-poll `browser_list_my_access_requests()` / retry nav to discover it. Should be PUSH: when the human approves a `browser_access_requests` row, emit a dashboard-style event to the REQUESTING agent ("user approved <origin> — retry now"), analogous to the `[DASHBOARD EVENT]` lines supervisors get. Decision point in the approve IPC (where the row → `browser_access_rules` upsert happens). Without it, agents silently downgrade to worse tools and the human's approval is wasted. Candidate: inject a one-line system notice into the requesting agent's input on approve (provider-appropriate), OR a structured event the agent's harness surfaces. Captured for the allowlist follow-up backlog.

=== 💡 V2 DESIGN CONCEPT (2026-06-16, user-raised — fold into v2 multi-supervisor migration planning) — distinguish "planning-surface workers" from "free workers" ===
Raised while herding the `transcript` worker (see behavioral.md B-21). Worth surfacing whenever v2 planning-surface architecture is discussed:
- **Two classes of worker should be first-class:** (1) a *free/directive worker* tackling a standalone user ask, vs (2) a *planning-surface worker* launched in service of a multi-phase plan.
- **They should emit results differently.** Free worker → may emit conversational "suggested next steps" to the supervisor (candidate; user unsure if too aggressive). Planning-surface worker → should NOT narrate next-steps; instead a **hook emits a STRUCTURED response that mutates the HTML planning surface following a template** (phase result, risks, test outcomes, chain-of-custody metadata: when/why/which groupthink).
- **Why:** for a plan-attached worker the supervisor is checking *how it did the work + is it good against the plan*, NOT looking for next-steps — so free-text next-steps from a plan worker are distracting noise. The next step is owned by the plan, not the worker.
- **Ties into the dictated v2 notes** (the transcript this session produced → `plans/v2-migration-dictated-notes-organized.md`): planning surface as directly-mutated HTML, chain-of-custody metadata on mutation, manager agent folding deliberations, suppressing internal groupthink events. The "structured template mutation by a plan-worker hook" is a concrete mechanism that serves several of those notes at once.
- **Status:** concept only — not designed, not built. When v2 planning-surface work is scoped, this distinction + the template-mutation hook should be an explicit design input.

=== 🐛 FIXED (2026-06-16, UNCOMMITTED, NOT LIVE) — unsupervised Windows claude WORKER launch crashed on inline --mcp-config ===
- **Symptom:** launching a plain (unsupervised) claude worker on Windows → `Error: Invalid MCP configuration: MCP config file not found: C:\...\.dashboard\workers\claude\"{""mcpServers""...}"`. Supervised/supervisor/researcher worked.
- **Root cause:** worker lane gets an inline `--mcp-config <JSON>` (toolsetsForLane('worker') non-empty). Reaching claude.exe via the `cmd.exe /c` path in scripts/pty-host.js (quoteForCmd doubles `"`→`""`, node-pty re-quotes) corrupts the JSON → claude treats it as a file path. The `directSpawn` gate at index.ts:~2610 listed isSupervisor||isSupervised||isResearcher but **OMITTED isWorker**, so workers fell through to cmd.exe. WSL path is safe (single-quotes the JSON).
- **Fix (worker 1d0bf306):** extracted a pure exported `shouldDirectSpawn()` helper in src/main/supervisor/mcp-config-builder.ts WITH isWorker added to the lane disjunction; index.ts gate now calls it (semantics identical for existing lanes). 6 regression tests in mcp-config-builder.test.ts; build:main clean; 30/30 pass. Only those 3 files touched. NO commit, NO restart.
- **NOT LIVE:** dist/ updated but running Electron main is the old binary → needs human `npm run restart` (supervisor must NOT restart — many live agents' PTYs would die). Then launch a plain unsupervised claude worker to verify. Commit is human's call (rides along with the big uncommitted allowlist/browser stack in this tree).

=== 📋 PLAN ON FILE — WEBSITE-ALLOWLIST POLICY for the embedded browser (`plans/website-allowlist-design.md`, APPROVED + AMENDED 2026-06-16, NOT yet implemented) ===

**AMENDMENT (§11–§16) APPROVED-WITH-CHANGES via GroupThink #2 (serial, runId f5c7cc36; Lead=claude 46bf6d8e, Reviewer=codex 660ed026). Review artifact = `plans/website-allowlist-amendment-review.md`. All required changes folded into §11–§16 of the main plan by supervisor this session.** Adds the user's HEADLINE feature: a per-site **"allow while signed in"** authenticated-drive tier so an agent can drive a site the user is logged into (e.g. LinkedIn job-hunting). This SUPERSEDES the old "List B = open-only, human is sole actor" stance for sites the user opts in.
- **Model:** one allowlist (Tier 1 = List A) + a per-row `allow_signed_in` boolean toggle. OFF = today's clean unauthenticated browsing; ON = agent may drive an authenticated session for that origin. (List B open-only handoff RETAINED for human-acts-only cases like OAuth.)
- **Mechanism A (PRIMARY, v1, NO M9 breach):** human signs in **directly inside a `persist:agent` tab** (login lands in the agent's own jar) → agent drives via the existing CDP path. NOT cookie-sharing (still rejected) — it's a fresh first-party login the human types (agent never sees password). **Sign-in QUARANTINE required (closes risk R6):** a visible persist:agent tab is agent-listable/drivable by default, so without quarantine the agent could screenshot/read the credential fields mid-entry. Fix = non-persisted `signinPending` TabEntry flag: while pending, `toolListTabs` omits it + `gate()` denies every verb (`PolicyError('signin-pending')`) + only trusted chrome closes it. Two trusted IPCs: `access-handoff-signin(ruleId)` opens the quarantined tab; `access-handoff-ready(tabId)` revalidates committed URL via `checkSignedInDrive` + upserts the origin + clears the flag.
- **Mechanism B (drive the human's live persist:user tab in place) = IN v1 per USER OVERRIDE** (user identified the "right-click my signed-in tab → hand to agent" gesture as THE headline feature; chose "fold B into v1 now, no extra GroupThink"). This OVERRIDES GroupThink #2's Q2 "A-only, defer B" recommendation. Crosses M9 (accepted eyes-open). **R2/R3 solved in §12-B/§14:** R2 (tabId delivery) = trusted right-click gesture gives main the tabId + `toolListTabs` predicate widened to `partition==='agent' || tab.handedToAgent` so a handed user tab is enumerable (un-handed user tabs stay invisible). R3 (checkAction purity) = manager-side `isAgentDrivable(tab) = partition==='agent' || (handedToAgent && checkSignedInDrive(committedUrl).allow)` routes attachDebugger/driver/gate through ONE helper (checkAction stays pure); `mayAttachDebugger` REPLACED by isAgentDrivable so CDP attaches to a handed user tab only while its origin stays allow_signed_in. Non-persisted `handedToAgent` TabEntry flag, set ONLY by trusted IPC `tab-hand-to-agent` (validates origin), cleared by `tab-return-to-human` OR auto-revoke (detach+clear on will-navigate/redirect/did-navigate off the allow_signed_in origin). Tab stays human-visible (consent surface) + "Agent driving" badge. Sensitive-origin act denial still non-overridable (handed sensitive-origin tab = read-only). **GAP: B got NO independent threat-model GroupThink — recommend a security review of the M9-relaxation diff before commit.** Impl pointers in §17.
- **Revocation (Q5):** `clearAgentSiteData` helper clears origin-scoped storage (`localstorage`/`indexdb`/`cachestorage` NOT `caches`, + `serviceworkers`) per **tracked** known-origins (new `browser_access_signed_in_origins` table — persists across restart since cookies do) PLUS domain-enumerated cookies (`ses.cookies.get({domain})` → construct removal URL from cookie.secure/domain/path → `ses.cookies.remove`). scheme:any clears BOTH https+http. Auto on rule delete; offered (not silent) on toggle OFF; per-row "Clear agent session" button.
- **Migration (Q3, CORRECTED):** house try/catch `ALTER TABLE browser_access_rules ADD COLUMN allow_signed_in INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` — my draft wrongly said "no migration precedent"; database.ts already does this 10+× (database.ts:78,89-106,337-352). No PRAGMA/version table.
- **Audit (Q5):** authenticated tool success (incl. read-tier getPageText/readPage/screenshot/waitFor which are NOT currently success-audited) → `ok:authenticated`.
- **Sensitive-origin (Q4):** act-tier denial stays NON-overridable even with allow_signed_in (read-tier allowed, act denied); no allowSensitive. **Base-policy fix:** make `closeTab` exposure-reducing (`EXPOSURE_REDUCING_VERBS = {goBack,goForward,closeTab}`) so close is always an escape on sensitive origins (today NAV_AWAY_VERBS = back/forward only → closeTab wrongly denied).
- **Q1 shared jar:** durable workspace-wide persist:agent login accepted for v1, gated on UI disclosure (four-point banner) + revocation + quarantine; per-task isolation deferred. **Q6 label:** "Let the agent use my signed-in session" (subcopy: shared by all agents, persists until cleared).
- **Infra ALL VERIFIED EXACT by GroupThink #2 against source** (browser-manager.ts:200-203/325-332/463-471/350/439-443; browser-decisions.ts:228-230; browser-policy.ts:283-344; cdp-driver.ts:193,202). Partition immutable post-creation; CDP partition-agnostic; only `mayAttachDebugger('persist:agent')` gate holds the agent off user tabs.
- **§18 ADDED (user request) — agent request-and-approve, IN v1.** A worker agent can call new tool `browser_request_site_access(hostname, reason, wantSignedIn?)` to REQUEST a site; it writes an INERT `browser_access_requests` pending row (grants ZERO access — does NOT touch checkAgentVisit/gates). Only a HUMAN approval in trusted chrome creates the real browser_access_rules row (Approve=visit-only allow_signed_in=0; "Approve + allow signed in"=separate louder grant). --- (original approved base, unchanged) ---
Final Lead+Reviewer consensus design (GroupThink serial, runId d6b73a51). Two human-curated lists layered on top of today's global on/off toggle + scheme/SSRF floor:
- **List A `agentAllow`** — origins the `persist:agent` partition may VISIT and have tools DRIVE. **TWO enforcement gates** (the Reviewer's key catches, both folded in): (1) navigation chokepoint — `will-navigate`/`will-redirect`/`createTab`/`navigate`, **top-level only** (subframes stay on the scheme floor; an origin allowlist on subframes would break real sites); (2) **existing-tab tool-use gate** — `gate(...)` re-checks the tab's committed URL on read/act verbs when `partitionFull==='persist:agent'` and mode is `enforce`, denying `agent-allowlist-denied`. Closes the stranded-tab cases (mode flip off→enforce, pre-policy tab, missed nav event). `closeTab`/`goBack`/`goForward`/`about:blank` exempt for recovery; `openUrl` keeps its target pre-check.
- **List B `humanHandoffAllow`** — the human's signed-in sites; agent gets **open-only** access via `openUrl({forHuman:true})` into `persist:user` (loads with the human's real cookies), but **M9 isolation holds verbatim** — no CDP attach, no getText/readPage, no act-tier, no tab enumeration. Human is the sole actor inside the authenticated session. Governs ONLY agent-initiated forHuman opens; normal human address-bar browsing is never restricted by this policy.
- **Security model:** cookie/session-sharing into `persist:agent` REJECTED outright (confused-deputy under prompt injection); authenticated agent access = the agent logging in with its OWN credentials in `persist:agent` = an ordinary List-A entry. `allowSensitive` per-rule override DROPPED from v1 (sensitive-origin act-denial stays non-overridable). Scheme+SSRF floor never overridable by either list.
- **Storage:** dashboard SQLite DB only — `browser_access_rules` (id/list/hostname/include_subdomains/scheme/path_prefix/note/enabled/created_at) + `browser_access_modes` (list PK, mode off|enforce). NOT agent-writable `.dashboard/` JSON. Hostname matching is origin/hostname-oriented (NOT eTLD+1 — avoids a public-suffix-list dep), `endsWith('.'+host)` subdomain guard, path-prefix normalized so `/app` ≠ `/apple`, port ignored in v1.
- **Cache:** `AccessPolicyCache` MUST be synchronously readable (`will-navigate`/`will-redirect` call `preventDefault()` inline, can't await; better-sqlite3 is sync). `setMode('agent','enforce')` invalidates cache + emits `accessChanged` + UI warns existing agent tabs may become blocked.
- **UI:** new `WebsiteAccessSettings.tsx` overlay modeled on `HistoryView` (`useBrowserSuspension()`), two sections (Agent / Human hand-off) each with mode toggle + add-rule form + per-row enable/delete; entry button next to `AgentActionsToggle`. Default modes `off/off` = compatibility (opt-in enforcement).
- **§10 file-change checklist + §9 tests are in the plan.** Supplementary input available: researcher survey at `.dashboard/research/inbox/agent-browser-allowlist-research/20260616T000000Z-agent-browser-allowlist-auth.md` (how browser-use/Playwright/Operator/computer-use/Puppeteer do allowlists + auth).
- **NEXT when user says go:** hand `plans/website-allowlist-design.md` to a Path-2 worker (scope src/main/browser/ + renderer browser components; gated build:main + browser suites; no .claude writes).

- **🎯 ROOT CAUSE (from trace decision lines, NOT theory):** On the 2nd httpbin type (replace:true): `valueLength:result branch:"direct" len:11` (value read CORRECTLY — DOMSnapshot saw the 11-char "claude code") → `clear:plan backspaces:13` (correctly planned to clear) → **yet field ended "claude codeanthropic" → the 13 Backspace key-events DELETED NOTHING.** So A2 is NOT value-source (DOMSnapshot works) and NOT wrapper-focus (resolveEditable correctly no-op'd `branch:"no-inner-match"` on a bare input, resolved to self). **It is: `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` Backspaces are NO-OPS in the embedded BrowserView, while `Input.insertText` (focus-independent) works → net APPEND.** Same mechanism explains A3 (Enter's default SUBMIT action no-ops) AND why A7 combobox worked (JS keydown LISTENERS fire fine; only native editing/submit DEFAULT-ACTIONS no-op). - **🔬 ROOT CAUSE SHARPENED by Phase-2 worker 2f1c6c58 (on-disk header comment):** agent tabs are **HIDDEN WebContentsViews** — browser-manager creates them `setVisible(false)` — so the renderer page is never "focused & active". On a hidden view, `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` editing/submit DEFAULT-ACTIONS (selectAll editor cmd, Backspace/Delete deletion, Enter implicit submit) silently NO-OP, while frame-level `Input.insertText` still lands → replace becomes append, Enter submits nothing. **Fix it implemented:** `assertPageFocused` → `Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled(true)` to hold page focused+active so default-actions fire; clear becomes focus-correct `selectAll` (named editor cmd via new SELECT_ALL in key-map) + `Input.insertText` (replaces selection), NO Backspace key-events.

=== RE-TEST 5 ADDENDUM (2026-06-16) — BUILD-LIVE PROOF + WEB RESEARCH PINS BOTH FIXES (Round-5 A3 hypothesis FALSIFIED) ===
- **BUILD WAS LIVE (proven, not assumed):** dist/main/main/browser/cdp-driver.js rebuilt 22:06:38 (source 21:41) WITH round-5 markers (CLEAR_BACKSPACE_OVERSHOOT, valueLength, Enter=single keyDown{text:'\r'}). Electron main PID 61500 started 22:06:58 — AFTER the build. **Process-start-time vs dist-mtime is the DEFINITIVE live-check — reuse it before ever blaming/clearing a fix.** So R5 failures are REAL, not stale-build.
- **WEB RESEARCH (general-purpose subagent, sourced: CDP docs, Chromium docs, Puppeteer source, devtools-protocol#45):**
  - **A3 root cause (FALSIFIES R5 theory):** Blink implicit form submission fires on the **default action of the DOM `keypress` event**, which in CDP is produced ONLY by a **`char`-type** event — NOT by `keyDown`. Our pressKey sends a SINGLE combined `keyDown{text:'\r'}` and NO char → no keypress → no submit. Puppeteer's Enter works because... it sets BOTH `text:'\r'` AND **`unmodifiedText:'\r'`** on the keyDown (we omit unmodifiedText), and/or Blink's keyDown→char disambiguation needs that. **FIX (durable): emit `keyDown{text:'\r', unmodifiedText:'\r'}` → explicit `char{text:'\r'}` → `keyUp` for char-producing keys.** This keeps key:'Enter' on the keydown (combobox commit stays green, A7 safe) AND adds the char that makes keypress (A3 submit). Use '\r' NOT '\n'. char-less keys (arrows/Delete/Backspace/End) stay rawKeyDown+keyUp. NOTE: this is the worker's pre-flagged "keyDown(with text)+char+keyUp belt-and-suspenders" lever — NOT round-3's broken rawKeyDown-no-text+char.
  - **A2 root cause:** `Accessibility.getFullAXTree` value (node.value.value) is **unreliable for `<input type=search>`** — search boxes expose as combobox/searchbox roles where AXValue is often empty/placeholder, NOT the typed value → valueLength returns 0 → 0 backspaces → append. **FIX: read input value length via `DOMSnapshot.captureSnapshot` `inputValue` field** (documented "Only set for input elements, contains the input's associated text value"; reflects live .value for text AND search; eval-free, M10-safe). Replace valueLength's getFullAXTree source with DOMSnapshot inputValue. (Secondary, also pre-flagged: descend DOM.focus from wrapper to inner <input> — but DOMSnapshot value source is the cleaner primary.)
  - Aside: Chrome 148 added a `frameId` requirement on getFullAXTree — another reason to move off AX for this.
- **NEXT:** run GroupThink deliberation seeded with above → plan → implement Round 6 (A3 char-event + A2 DOMSnapshot value). Re-test MUST re-verify A7 (Enter payload changes again) + A3 on httpbin POST + A2 on all 3 sites.

- **ROOT-CAUSE DIG DONE (2× parallel general-purpose, READ-ONLY, 2026-06-15). Both fixes minimal + M10-safe (no eval):**
  - **A3 root cause:** CDP `rawKeyDown` DROPS any text and never makes a DOM keypress; the standalone `char{'\r'}` event routes through Blink's text-INSERTION path (no-op for '\r' in single-line input), NOT the form default-submit action which keys off the Enter keydown/keypress VK. So round-3's split (rawKeyDown-no-text → char → keyUp) fires neither submit path. Old single `keyDown{text:'\r'}` worked because it made keydown + inline keypress w/ Enter VK. **The combobox rationale for the split was WEAK:** a JS keydown handler reading event.key==='Enter' sees an IDENTICAL keydown with or without inline text (text only affects a later keypress, doesn't pre-empt the keydown handler that already ran) → re-test-3 combobox fail was FOCUS-LOSS, not char-preemption. **FIX:** in pressKey(), for keys WITH text emit a SINGLE `keyDown{...common, text}` then `keyUp{...common}` (no text); char-less keys stay rawKeyDown+keyUp. Lands cdp-driver.ts:286-303; update 2 Enter asserts in cdp-driver.test.ts. ⚠️ ONE change fixes A3 AND should keep A7 (verify with CLEAN back-to-back click-open→ArrowDown→Enter + httpbin form submit in same pass).
  - **A2 root cause:** `commands:['selectAll']` runs against the FRAME's focused editable; on type=search (Wikipedia)/DDG, DOM.focus(backendNodeId) likely lands on a WRAPPER (combobox/role=search container) not the inner <input> → no editable selection → selectAll+Delete no-op → insertText appends. type=search is NOT editor-distinct from type=text in Blink — the split is purely which node got focused. **FIX (durable primitive):** AX-length backspaces — a11y-snapshot.ts:148 exposes node.value.value WITHOUT eval (M10-safe); pass value/length into typeText opts; replace path = focus → (optional selectAll+Delete fast path) → End → Backspace × (len + ~2 overshoot; harmless no-ops on empty) → insertText. Uses existing NAMED_KEYS.End/Backspace. Overshoot is safe (extra backspaces on empty = no-op); UTF-16 .length safe-by-overshoot. Recommend End+Backspace×len as the primary, selectAll optional.
- **✅ FIX ROUND 5 DONE + GREEN (bc08ae24, UNCOMMITTED, 2026-06-15).** Both root-caused fixes landed in src/main/browser/: (A3) pressKey reverted — key WITH text → single keyDown{...common,[commands],text}→keyUp (no char); char-less keys unchanged rawKeyDown→keyUp. (A2) typeText replace path = selectAll+Delete (fast path) → End → Backspace×(len+CLEAR_BACKSPACE_OVERSHOOT=2) → insertText; new private valueLength(backendNodeId) reads AX value length via getFullAXTree (eval-free, M10-safe). Tests: cdp-driver 15 (Enter=2-event keyDown+text→keyUp asserting NO char; command-rides-keydown-only; replace=selectAll+Delete+End+Backspace×(5+2=7)+insertText), key-map 5, browser-policy 35, a11y-snapshot 8, api-browser-routes 22, mcp-browser-tools 24, browser-decisions 16; build:main clean. acceptance doc A2 updated. WORKER LIVE-ONLY LEVERS: (A3) if Enter still no-submit, Chromium may not emit keypress from a CDP keyDown-with-text → fallback = keyDown(with text)+char+keyUp belt-and-suspenders, or submit-button click (already works, buttons:1); (A2) if a site still appends → stale AX length (re-read before clear) OR Backspace not reaching inner input → descend focus from wrapper to inner <input>; (A7) reverting the split should keep A7 green (round-3 fail was focus-loss).

Symptom: Enter not committing after click-open — DOM focus likely not landing on the combobox/listbox post-CDP-click, OR Enter delivered as form-submit variant (text '\r') the widget ignores vs rawKeyDown. POSSIBLE KNOWN CDP LIMITATION (click→focus→key sequencing).
- **ROOT-CAUSE DIG DONE (2× parallel general-purpose, read-only, 2026-06-16). User chose "root-cause first, no dispatch." BOTH are fixable tooling bugs, M10-safe, NOT CDP limitations:**
  - **A2 root cause:** the clear sequence (cdp-driver.ts:257-262) dispatches Shift+Ctrl+Home as a plain `rawKeyDown` with NO CDP `commands:[]` array. Chromium only runs editing/selection commands (MoveToBeginningOfDocumentAndModifySelection) when keydown carries an explicit `commands:['…']` array — it does NOT infer them from VK+modifiers. Plain `<input type=text>` honors caret keys by default; `<input type=search>` + DDG swallow/reinterpret the synthetic keydown → no selection forms → Delete no-ops → insertText appends. Bitmask (Ctrl+Shift=10) is CORRECT, ruled out. **FIX:** replace 3-key clear with a single `Input.dispatchKeyEvent {type:'rawKeyDown', key:'a', code:'KeyA', vk65, modifiers:2, commands:['selectAll']}` + keyUp → Delete → insertText. `commands:['selectAll']` is a NAMED edit command (NOT JS eval, M10-OK), input-agnostic. AX value IS available without eval (a11y-snapshot.ts:148,159 `node.value.value`) so Backspace×len is a viable secondary. Lands cdp-driver.ts:257-262; assert `commands` in cdp-driver.test.ts:55-92.

So ArrowDown (no text) → clean rawKeyDown → widget moves activedescendant; Enter → keyDown carrying `text:'\r'` → Chrome also generates keypress/beforeinput char, which pre-empts the combobox's keydown `event.key==='Enter'` commit path. A real keyboard Enter on a combobox is a plain keydown/keyup, no char. Both edges ARE sent (ruled out (b)/(d)); click DOES move focus (ArrowDown worked → ruled out (c)). **FIX:** dispatch Enter as rawKeyDown (clean, satisfies combobox keydown handler) → separate `char` event text:'\r' (preserves A3 implicit form-submit) → keyUp. Lands key-map.ts:23 + cdp-driver.ts:289-294. ⚠️ Enter path is SHARED with A3 form-submit (which PASSES) → round-3 re-test MUST re-verify A3 too, not just A2+A7.
  - Context note: researcher e8f5ce15 hit dashboard 100%/242K but that's vs the 200K false ceiling (real 1M ≈ 24% used) — still reusable.
- **✅ FIX ROUND 3 DONE + GREEN (bc08ae24, UNCOMMITTED, 2026-06-16).** Both root-caused fixes landed in src/main/browser/: (A2) key-map.ts SELECT_ALL now `{key:'a',code:'KeyA',vk65,modifiers:2,commands:['selectAll']}` (CdpKey gained `commands?:string[]`; unused CARET_TO_END/SELECT_TO_START removed); typeText replace path = pressKey(SELECT_ALL)→pressKey(Delete)→insertText; pressKey appends `commands` only on keydown edge. (A7/Enter) pressKey now splits char-producing keys into rawKeyDown(NO text)→char{text:'\r'}→keyUp(NO text); char-less keys (ArrowDown/Delete/Escape/selectAll-keydown) unchanged = rawKeyDown+keyUp. **⚠️ OPS NOTE (2026-06-14) — DASHBOARD CONTEXT-THRESHOLD EVENTS ARE FALSE ALARMS FOR 1M-CONTEXT WORKERS.** Wave-1 workers run on "Opus 4.8 (1M context)". The dashboard fires 80%/90% "compact this agent" events computed against a hardcoded `contextWindowMax: 200000` (get_context_stats), but the worker's REAL window is 1M — at ~181K the worker's own footer reads "Context: 82% remaining". So those threshold events are spurious (~18% actually used). Verify against the WORKER'S OWN footer ("Context: N% remaining") or get_context_stats totalContextTokens vs 1M, NOT the dashboard percentage, before ever compacting. Do NOT yank a working 1M worker on an 80/90% dashboard event. (Possible dashboard bug: contextWindowMax should track the 1M model variant.)

**🔬 2026-06-12 ~10:15 — WSL-CODEX RESIDUAL BUGS DIAGNOSED (post-clean-restart, codex 0.139 on both WSL+Windows; app launched outside claude so env-poisoning cleared).** User reports two WSL-only residuals: (a) codex crashes ~2/6 on WSL; (b) launching two codex agents near-simultaneously cross-binds their chats (type into one, reply lands in the other's pane). Windows clean. Investigation:
- **CROSS-BINDING (root-caused):** DB proves it — agents `af9d7b8d` "codex again" AND `e1f1b329` "codex why 2" both carry sid `019ebcbe-47fa-7921-b155-dc23b337a660` (one rollout, two agents). Mechanism: **ALL codex workers in a workspace share ONE cwd** (`<ws>/.dashboard/workers/codex` — same on Windows too, verified). Codex defers its rollout + `state_5.sqlite threads`-row creation until it processes the FIRST USER MESSAGE, not at launch. The dashboard's sibling disambiguation in that shared cwd is `first_user_message` prefix ONLY (`discoverNewCodexSession`, session-id-discovery.ts:260 SQL `cwd LIKE % AND created_at>=launch AND first_user_message LIKE prefix%`). When two same-cwd agents launch close together and get short/identical prompts ("ping"), cwd is identical and prefix is identical → ambiguous/sibling-steal → one agent binds the other's rollout; reader tails the shared file → reply appears in wrong pane. **Why WSL-only in practice:** Windows test agents flushed rollout ~1s after launch (sid_ts≈created_at+1s for all 6) because prompts auto-submit fast; WSL agent `af9d7b8d` had an **80s gap** (created 16:49:27, rollout 16:50:47) because the human typed the first message late — that long deferred window is exactly when a sibling launch cross-binds. Freshness guard (`selectFreshCodexRollouts`, launch−2s floor) and SQL `created_at>=launch` SHOULD reject a 10s-older sibling rollout, yet the collision still landed → the exact binding path needs a worker's deeper trace, but the structural cause (shared cwd + deferred rollout + prefix-only tiebreak) is certain. **FIX DIRECTION: give each codex agent its OWN cwd** (`workers/codex/<agentId>`) so cwd becomes a unique discriminator — kills ambiguity AND sibling-steal outright; far more robust than tightening the prefix/freshness heuristics.
- **CRASH 2/6 (WSL-only, partially diagnosed):** launch diagnostic (`<ws>/.dashboard/launches.log`) for crashed `08444cd9`/`fcf75564` shows `tmux new-session -d` exit 0 then dashboard `tmux attach` fails ~2s later with `can't find session` → **codex exited within ~2s of startup, before any rollout/threads-row** (neither appears in state_5.sqlite). My repro (6× serial AND 6× concurrent detached-tmux launches of the identical `ccodex … --profile dashboard-worker …` command) → ALL survived, so it's NOT a pure tmux race; it's codex itself intermittently dying on startup under WSL. Prime suspect: concurrent-startup contention on the shared `~/.codex` SQLite WALs (`logs_2.sqlite` is **141 MB**, `state_5.sqlite`) when multiple codex boot at once — SQLITE_BUSY/lock on cold open. Per-agent cwd would NOT fix this (the SQLite files are global to ~/.codex); needs a separate look (retry-on-startup-crash, or codex-side lock backoff). `ccodex` = bash fn: `source ~/.venvs/claude-env/bin/activate && codex "$@"`. Not yet root-caused to a single error string — worker should capture codex stderr on a crashing boot (my synchronous repro kept getting auto-backgrounded by the harness; a worker in a normal shell can loop-capture).
- **NEXT:** offered the user a worker to (1) move codex workers to per-agent cwd (cross-binding fix) and (2) instrument/repro the startup crash. Awaiting go.


**🚨🚨🚨 SUPERSEDING ROOT CAUSE (2026-06-12 ~08:50 local) — TODAY'S DEAD CHAT/CONTEXT = ENV POISONING, NOT (just) RESUME-ORPHANING. FULL DOC: `docs/BUG_claude-child-session-env-poisoning.md`.** The app's 07:30 + 08:27 boots were run via `npm run restart` typed **inside the user's terminal claude session** (proven by Win32_Process ancestry: electron ← npm ← powershell ← claude.exe PID 15240). With claude 2.1.175 (auto-updated 06-11 22:11) + `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS=1` (added to `~/.claude/settings.json` env 06-11 10:06), every dashboard-spawned Windows claude inherits `CLAUDECODE=1`/`CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION=1`/`CLAUDE_CODE_SSE_PORT` → acts as a NESTED CHILD session → **writes only an ai-title stub (or nothing) to its projects .jsonl — conversations NOT persisted at all** → reader has nothing to tail → no chat events, no context stats, AND those sessions can't be resumed-with-history later. WSL immune (tmux server env doesn't inherit Electron's env — why "WSL claude works"); user's own terminal claude immune (clean env); `claude -p` exempt (print mode persists even with poisoned env). **CODEX SIBLING BUG (same dig):** WSL codex 0.135.0 blocks at an interactive update prompt (0.135→0.139 "Press enter") → handshake fail → the observed crash/restart ×2 → no rollout written → cwd-match sid recovery bound agent c6a710ea to a JUNE 4 rollout = "attached to old codex chat" (phantom 86%/15-turn ctx). Windows codex 0.136.0 = same race milder: fresh rollout exists (08-28-11-019ebc72) but DB bound the 00:11 AM one (019ebaab) → user msgs show (synthetic bridge echo) but assistant replies never (reader tails dead rollout). UUIDv7-timestamp-decode of codex sids vs agent created_at is the instant tell. **✅ CODE FIX IMPLEMENTED (worker `c61b07fc`, 2026-06-12 ~09:15, UNCOMMITTED; build:main + full test:supervisor GREEN; worker retired).** Authoritative record = `docs/fix-progress-env-poisoning.md` (worker chat NOT readable — it ran poisoned). **🐞🐞🐞 LIVE BUG FOUND (2026-06-12 ~08:00Z) — CHAT + CONTEXT STATS DON'T UPDATE LIVE; ROOT-CAUSED, UNFIXED.** Human dogfooding the wave on a clean `npm run restart` reported: clicking an OLD agent renders full chat HISTORY but new user msgs + agent responses never append to the chat pane (terminal view DOES show them); NEW agents have NO context bar at all; old agents' context bar is frozen. **Bisected with the human:** (1) renderer `onChatEvents` listener registered via DevTools console — sending a ping printed NOTHING → main process is not forwarding live `agent:chat-events`. (2) Context stats (same `SessionLogReader`/`ContextStatsMonitor` source) also frozen/absent → not chat-specific, it's the reader's live tail. **ROOT CAUSE (confirmed on disk):** the dispatcher (`session-log-dispatcher.ts`) polls each agent's file via `getActiveAgentSessions()` which derives the path from the DB `resumeSessionId`. But Claude **forks a NEW session `.jsonl` (new uuid) on RESUME instead of appending** — and the dashboard keeps the OLD id in the DB. Proof: agent `7b61ee41` DB `resume_session_id=34ee4c5b…` but it's actively writing `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-turke-Projects-AgentDashboard--dashboard-workers-claude/b2d8e129-….jsonl` (mtime 07:50:45, = when it last replied). Reader tails the stale `34ee4c5b` file → 0 new events every poll → no `chat-events`, no `usage`. App restarted at 07:30 → every resumed agent is now orphaned from its live file. **The wave is NOT the cause** — wave touched none of `session-log-dispatcher.ts` / `session-log-reader.ts` / readers / preload / `ChatPane` subscribe path (all verified unchanged in git diff); the +8 ipc-handlers & +69 supervisor/index.ts edits are additive (WP-P2 pending-prompt + sendInputError forward). This is a pre-existing resume/session-id reconciliation gap, surfaced by the 07:30 restart. **FIX DIRECTION (for a worker — main process, I can't edit code):** on claude resume, detect the forked session file and rebind — there's already machinery: `SessionLogDispatcher.rebindAgent(agentId)` + `invalidatePath` + the codex `recoverCodexResumeSessionId`/`maybeRecoverCodexSid` cwd-match pattern (supervisor/index.ts ~2500-2590). Claude needs an analogous "newest .jsonl in project dir written after launch wins" reconciliation that updates `resumeSessionId` in DB + calls `rebindAgent`. Verify by: after fix, message a resumed agent → chat pane appends + context bar moves. **FULL WRITEUP: `docs/BUG_session-log-resume-orphaned-session-file.md`** (symptom, bisection, on-disk proof, why-now hypotheses, proposed fix mirroring codex `recoverCodexResumeSessionId`+`rebindAgent`, shared-dir hijack caution, acceptance tests, file pointers). **NEXT: launch ONE focused worker against this (do NOT bundle with wave commit). Human was mid-dogfood; this blocks the dogfood/commit decision.** Inventory note: human's test agents `0431fd5e`(test)/`da5718aa`(test 2) have `context:null`; most post-restart agents show model `<synthetic>` 0% = same orphaning. Supervisor (me, `2d5d1eee`) hit 98% ctx during this dig — may need compaction.

- [groupthink-premature-stall-no-plan-written.md](groupthink-premature-stall-no-plan-written.md) - **OPS LESSON 2026-06-11.** GroupThink (parallel) fired `orchestration.groupthink.stalled` `reason:no_plan_written` `resume_hint:null` while the synthesizer was STILL writing — the stall detector raced, treating the synthesizer's R3 *reconciliation* turn-complete as round-done and checking the disk too early. File appeared ~minutes later; run completed cleanly. **Recovery recipe: on `no_plan_written`, check the DISK (`ls planPath`) and the PTY (spinner = still working) before acting; do NOT `send_message` to a working synthesizer (handshake will reject / you'll race the write); poll for the file in the background instead; only nudge if file truly absent AND agent idle/done.** `status:working` post-stall is often genuine, not stale.

- [codex-hook-trust-persistence.md](codex-hook-trust-persistence.md) - Whether rebuild / reopen / new-dir resets Codex hook trust. Verified 2026-05-29: plain rebuild + same dir does NOT reset; new dir doesn't carry persisted trust but the `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust` launch flag runs hooks anyway; the only real silent re-gate is editing the hook command/script in constants.ts + a scaffold version bump. Trust lives in `~/.codex/config.toml [hooks.state]`, keyed per-workspace-config-path + command hash.
